ParlaMint-IT_2015-01-22-LEG17-Senato-sed-382.ana.u12	MUCCHETTI (PD). Mr President, yesterday I tried to table a reworded text of my amendments on the causes of ineligibility, which were declared impossible under an article in the Rules of Procedure which decrees extraneousness on the ground. Text 2 was based on the fact that I had already tabled these amendments in the first Commission, but due to a material error they had not been included in the file. I have been told that it is no longer possible to present them. However, I wanted to raise the question of the Presidency's decision on the extraneousness of the causes of Members' ineligibility when it comes to electoral law. I rely on your opening.
ParlaMint-IT_2015-01-22-LEG17-Senato-sed-382.ana.u13	Senator Cucchetti, the problem has already been raised yesterday and widely discussed with speeches by my colleague Crimi. The impproposability of these amendments has been judged to be such both in the Commission and in this House, so the Presidency has decided.
ParlaMint-IT_2015-01-22-LEG17-Senato-sed-382.ana.u14	I'm asking to talk.
ParlaMint-IT_2015-01-22-LEG17-Senato-sed-382.ana.u15	You have the right to do so.
ParlaMint-IT_2015-01-22-LEG17-Senato-sed-382.ana.u16	CRIMI (M5S). Mr President, I am sorry, but the problem has not been widely discussed. I have asked and have been answered in two laconic words: 'It is improbable'. There was no discussion. I would also point out that there has not been any discussion in the Commission either, since it has not been declared unpropos or foreclosures or anything else, because the work in that place has been closed, I agree with Senator Mucchetti's words, because, among other things, I myself pointed out the question yesterday: why, under an electoral law, do we not deal with the causes of ineligibility, incompatibility or anything else? Why? I would also like to make one point which I have made in the Commission. This law, in our view, is not, in the strict sense, an electoral law, but a modification, as is the case in practice, of the legislative stratification that has occurred over the years in the electoral field. The law under consideration, in fact, provides for a series of changes and interventions on all the electoral laws that have followed over the years and therefore it is not an organic electoral law to which it can be said that this is part of the matter and that it is not part of it. This is a set of changes to all election laws. Therefore, there is no reason for extraneousness in adding a change or integration to a law that is closely linked to an electoral law or that in any case falls within the electoral sphere. I would therefore like to reiterate my objection regarding impproposability. I know that it will not lead to anything because the Presidency's decision is unappellable. I believe, however, that it should remain in the record. Mr President, they point out to me that even during the examination of the constitutional reform we had tried to discuss some rules relating to eligibility, incompatibility or incandidateness, but they were deleted and declared inadmissible because of the extraneous nature of the matter because they concerned the electoral law. Perhaps I should retrieve the shorthand of those days when we were told that these matters were related to the electoral law. If I get it back, I really want to see if we're going to give further evidence of an abandoned house in which every rule is exonerated. [[Applause from the M5S Group.]]

ParlaMint-IT_2015-01-22-LEG17-Senato-sed-382.ana.u19	MUCCHETTI (PD). Mr President, I agree with what has just been said by Senator Crimi and note that the text 2 of these amendments which I tabled in a Commission which had not finished its work (and in which I do not know that there has been a broad debate on the subject) was in practice the transcription of a draft law which I had tabled at the beginning of the legislature - with the second signature of the PD leader to the Zanda Senate and the third of Vice-President Fedeli, and so on - and which was a proposal quite similar to that which you yourself, Mr Calderoli, presented with Senator Bisinella. I believe that we have a problem of a really very marked gravity: the laws that regulate, from the fifties, the incandidability, the ineligibility and the incompatibility did not foresee, as was structured the economy of Italy half a century ago, causes of incompatibility or ineligibility related to the condition of controlling shareholder, held by a parliamentarians, in companies participated by the State, or in companies that operate under concession or licence or otherwise in regulated sectors. Colleagues, do you realize that, saying no to these issues, without a debate but with the trap of the foreignness of matter in the constitutional law, and also saying no on the same subject in the electoral law, could we elect a state concessionaire - for example - for a motorway concession? PRESIDENT. - The debate is closed. Senator Cows, we're talking about a matter of eligibility. Let's not get into this right now. MUCCHETTI (PD). All right, so I ask the Presidency and you, Mr President, that you think exactly like me on this point, as well as colleagues in the PD who have signed a bill of this kind, in the name of what we put our heads under the sand, without giving any justification, at the time when for the first time we can vote on a measure that solves this problem? Here we are all adults: there are really clear eligibility or non-eligibility profiles and there are a little more optional ones, and here we are exercising the ability to put our heads under the sand. I ask why this is happening, and you tell me, Mr President, but give me a political speech and not a bureaucrat, because I do not recognize it - I say it without any offense, but with all the esteem that deserves the qualification of a politician - that I am a person who solves a political problem by fleeing from the assumption of responsibility. I add - and I say this to so much of this House - that, when the bicameral Commission was set up in the 1990s, which was to proceed with constitutional reform, that initiative, which had its great ambitions, was brandished as an incursion and its protagonists were hanged in that image, also because, it was said, on the altar of that agreement - which then did not manifest itself - the resolution of the issues relating to conflicts of interest was sacrificed. Now, the amending proposal which is not considered admissible does not have a general ambition on the issue of conflicts of interest. It is a proposal that is absolutely aimed at determining who is eligible or not. Do you realize that, at this stage, we could have a President of the Council who is a concessionaire of the State, with the conscious blessing of a Chamber that, if he did so, would flee to his own responsibilities? If it's okay with you, not in my name. [[Applause from the PD, M5S and Misto-SEL groups and Senators Gambaro, Simeoni and Di Maggio.]]

ParlaMint-IT_2015-01-22-LEG17-Senato-sed-382.ana.u31	CRIMI (M5S). Mr President, I would like to take advantage of the vote on this amendment to raise two issues. First of all, I would like to ask the Presidency once again to give us the time to open the tome and read the page on which the amendment is based and to check what is written, even if I understand that most of us in this House are voting automatically; we too, in spite of ourselves, are following this technique whereby, if the Government expresses a favourable opinion, we can only vote against it, and vice versa, because we do not have the time materially to read the content of the amendment and consider whether it is worth spending even a few words to support it. I would like to make a second point on the subject. We had asked from the beginning to know in advance - and before the completion of the work in the Commission, but alas, how it went - before the law was passed, the exact distribution of colleges and districts. Regardless of the electoral model, we know - and it is well known and well known - that the distribution of colleges is one of the most important and sensitive issues in the context of an electoral law. When it is said that in the details of the electoral law the trap is hidden, one of the ways to hide, camouflage the traps is that of the electoral colleges. The electoral colleges - and this has been confirmed by our colleagues in the majority in the Commission - are often determined according to the electoral body in such a way as to predetermine, on the basis of historical data, one result rather than another. It is useless to hide it, because it is obvious and known to all that there are so-called armored colleges in which the candidate of a certain coalition, of a certain political part, is certain to be elected. We have armored colleges left and right, and this has always involved, even the distribution of applications. In this case, with the capilists blocked, let's remember, you can run in these colleges who absolutely want to bring to Parliament, and therefore the whole family of the renziana, understood as an enlarged political family, placing these people as capilist in the colleges that will be armored in the sense that they will have the certainty to take that seat or perhaps more than one and in this case the co-optation of the second, chosen with preferences that will still be fictitious because it will always be dragged by the head coach. So the determination of the colleges, ladies and gentlemen, is fundamental, and I think it is also important for you to know now what the colleges will be in order to then be able to consciously vote an electoral law. I repeat again now the request that we made with agendas and requests to the Commission on more than one occasion also here in the Chamber, in the context of the preliminary and suspensive question, to know the exact location of the colleges, at national and local level in the districts, before proceeding with the final adoption of this law. I think that's common sense. We cannot pass a law that speaks of attached tables without knowing exactly the colleges and then leave them to the determination of the Ministry. This means leaving them to the determination of the Government and therefore to the determination of the majority currently to the Government that can do and undo, to do the beautiful and the bad time to obtain the best distribution of colleges in view of the best result. An electoral law should not be made and voted in this way, leaving wide delegation to the Ministry to manipulate with the colleges the real result that will come later. Therefore we reiterate the request to the Ministry, in particular to the Undersecretary Pizzetti since the Minister is engaged to chat in a corner, given also his tenacious and constant presence in the performance of the work (and we acknowledge it although I hope it is not only because it serves a vote but precisely because of his passion for the task he is carrying out), to be able to know as soon as possible the colleges, before the vote. I think it's a demand for common sense that has nothing instrumental, obstructive or anything else. [[Applause from the M5S Group.]]

ParlaMint-IT_2015-01-22-LEG17-Senato-sed-382.ana.u76	DE PETRIS (Misto-SEL). Mr President, we consider this to be a very important amendment, because it deals with one of the issues that - as you know - we have raised several times, focusing on it in many of our speeches and requiring greater responsibility. I also recall Mr De Cristofaro's speech on this very subject. With this amendment, we ask that the Premier's de facto statement be deleted, because we believe that he is being introduced through the electoral law - I also say this to those who have said that, through the electoral law, we do not make constitutional changes, but in reality this is the classic example of how surreptitiously, in the text proposed by the majority group leaders, we are coming to a real change in the form of government - almost the element of a direct election and, therefore, a direct designation of the Premier himself. This is to such an extent that this element is strengthened by the same ballot - the systems of balloting are those that are used in general, and this does not only concern our country through the direct election of the mayor, when there is a monocratic office, I think of France and the direct election of the President of the Republic - determining in the facts of a constitutional change. We have always said that, if this is the choice of the majority, it was necessary to assume the responsibility of making proposals for constitutional change, which would have allowed at least to put in place - as should be done in these cases - all the elements of counterweight, if we want to leave Parliament still the possibility of having a power. In some ways we have also indicated one of the dangers of this electoral law, one from the issues that not only do not convince us, but on which we are absolutely opposed: not only are we weakening parliamentary democracy, but in fact we are producing a change; it changes that I myself understand, President, and that in the system we have already seen in some way, when we have come to the choice of putting the name on the card. And this also happened with the Mattarellum. But precisely because we discussed a constitutional reform in the Senate - which is now under discussion in the House - I believe that a real proposal for a change would have been necessary, which would have allowed all the fundamental elements to be put into place in a democratic system of counterweights. On the contrary, in this way, with an electoral law, we create a real change in the form of government and, therefore, a constitutional change. [[Applause from the Misso-SEL Group.]]

ParlaMint-IT_2015-01-22-LEG17-Senato-sed-382.ana.u80	ZELLER (Aut (SVP, UV, PATT, UPT)-PSI-MAIE). Mr President, I will withdraw Amendment No 1 7000/68, but I would like to point out to my colleagues that there is a delicate problem of constitutionality and also some contradiction in this text. In fact, the Camera text, in compliance with a precise provision to protect the Slovenian linguistic minority, requires that in the Friuli Venezia Giulia district one of the multi-name colleges should be designated taking into account the presence of the Slovenian linguistic minority. This is already a very positive step forward, but the problem of the threshold remains because, as colleagues know, linguistic minorities must not exceed the thresholds set at national level, but must respect the thresholds of 20% set at regional level. If we establish a favorable measure for Slovenians, providing for a college designated according to the residence of this linguistic minority, it would also be logical to bring the 20% threshold to that specially defined college. It is a bit absurd that we should leave the 20% threshold at regional level, where it is mathematically impossible for the Slovenian linguistic minority to reach that percentage. In other words, even if the Slovenian linguistic minority were able to win one of the seats of the multi-national college, it would be excluded because it would not reach the threshold at regional level. I have raised this problem, I realize that there is no political majority in this House to approve this amendment and then withdraw it. However, I warn my fellow Members that the text risks being unconstitutional on this point. [[Applause from the Aut Group (SVP, UV, PATT, UPT) - PSI-MAIE.]]

ParlaMint-IT_2015-01-22-LEG17-Senato-sed-382.ana.u110	FINOCCHIAR (PD). Mr President, I have considered the text of this amendment and would propose a technical correction. This is the so-called anti-flipper rule, which we discussed so much about in the Commission and during the last debate on electoral reform. In the text published I believe that a technical correction should be made, which I would first read and then give you a copy, because it seems to me that the procedure by which the procedure for allocating seats is carried out, in order to avoid, or rather limit as much as possible, the flipper effect, is not complete. I would propose the following technical correction. Without prejudice to the incipit, i.e. to paragraph 17, Chapter "Art. 83- bis ', paragraph 1, number 4', I request that the second sentence be replaced by the following: If not, it shall determine the list which has the highest number of surplus seats and, in the same way as these, the list which has obtained the surplus seat with the lowest decimal part of the quotient; it shall therefore subtract the seat from that list in that college and assign it to the deficit list which has the largest number of deficit seats and, in the same way as these, to the list which has the largest decimal part of the quotient which has not given rise to the seat allocation; the seat shall be assigned to the deficit list in that multi-annual college; it shall repeat, in succession, such operations until the allocation of all surplus seats to the deficit lists.' It seems to me that this new text, which I give to the Presidency, better grasps the need that came from the debate in the Commission to avoid or, at least, limit as much as possible the allocation of seats that would shift the allocation of seats, also, in all probability - you yourself made an example during the general discussion in the Commission - a person who had obtained a large number of preferences in favour of a person who, on the other hand, had not reported such number of preferences and to whom the seat was assigned, as a result of this effect, as is said in the technical language, of "splitting" the allocation itself.
ParlaMint-IT_2015-01-22-LEG17-Senato-sed-382.ana.u111	Colleagues, the pinball problem is absolutely real. I myself, in the Commission, had feared the risk that, while limiting it, it would happen. I understand the technical reasons, but, in order to be able to deepen the text that has been read to us, since it is an additional amendment, I have a provision, so that the Presidency and colleagues can read it and fully reason on it. Being additional doesn't take away anything and we can move on; then we will return, but having confidence in what we are talking about.

ParlaMint-IT_2015-01-22-LEG17-Senato-sed-382.ana.u118	I'm asking to talk.
ParlaMint-IT_2015-01-22-LEG17-Senato-sed-382.ana.u119	You have the right to do so.
ParlaMint-IT_2015-01-22-LEG17-Senato-sed-382.ana.u120	CRIMI (M5S). Mr President, it confirms to me that there will be a suspension so that this issue can be deepened. However, I would like to take advantage of this opportunity because I believe it is necessary to contribute to the debate on this subject in order to allow colleagues, even in their sub-emerging and deepening activities, to be able to assess this amendment specifically, in substance. I tried to do some simulations, approximate for reasons of time. The sub-amendment to our examination deals with issues related to the so-called flipper effect, namely the assignment of a candidate to a different college than that to which it would belong. This effect is the result of a particularly complex calculation which starts from the National Central Office. This office assigns to each central circumscription office a total of seats and determines the percentage of each list for that office. After that, when it comes to assigning seats to each list and in each multi-annual college, the pinball effect can be created. In fact, the amendment to our examination does not solve the pinball effect, on the contrary, it is mathematically perfect, in the sense that it works both from the regulatory and technical point of view (but I had no doubt about the correctness) but in my opinion (and I ask colleagues to verify it), it could even increase the possibility of pinball effect. In fact, after a first distribution, although the surplus seats come out in a specific college, when the deficit college is determined in relation to that list, the deficit is determined by a first calculation which, at the time it is corrected, may in some cases even see the initial situation worse. In the first distribution between colleges we already go to identify the college that has had the best result compared to that list and, therefore, the one where voters have most wanted to reward that list. At the time that the surplus seat was removed from that list to attribute it to the list of the neighbouring college, which perhaps did not have even a seat, but simply because the voters of that college did not want to reward that list in that college, this would benefit from the towing from a neighbouring college, where perhaps that list had a prize in terms of excessive votes as it related to the blindfolding (because there is also this effect and here the question of the importance of having colleges first returns), and it would create a greater flipper effect. I do not believe that this sub-amendment solves the problem, and I do not think that any kind of technical solution does so. The problem is precisely the type of assignment that is made in a college. And I am referring to the intra-circumscription flipper effect, because then we will have to talk about the flipper effect between the circuits, which is not solved by any amendment or sub-amendment. The intra-circumscription flipper effect is not solved by this amendment: on the contrary. I appreciate the willingness to address this issue and I do not want to challenge this effort but, in my opinion, this sub-amendment, like any technical solution you try to find, only makes the situation of the flipper effect worse. I urge you to think carefully about the entire distribution of seats. I do not invite you to think about the majority prize, the thresholds, the list and coalition prize, which are political issues that you have now addressed and decided on. I call on you to take a serious and thorough look at the way in which seats are distributed, which is not acceptable under these conditions. This method identifies a circumscription figure of assigned seats, which is then further redistributed to colleges. That's not how it works at the moment. In the current law there is a circuit, a list throughout the circuit and 10 seats assigned to that list. The 10 with the most votes, as we would like, but in any case the first 10 are elected. Here, instead, we will have 10 seats assigned, in the Lombardy 2 district, to the list of the 5 Star Movement. These will have to be distributed among the various colleges that will be present in that circuit. This further distribution is deleterious, because it causes that effect of territorial non-representativeity. So we can find all the technical solutions we want, but we will not be able to solve this problem and we will have really aberrant situations. Some territories will again be neglected and left to themselves. I remind you that there are some huge circumscriptions in which, in the end, those who determine the election of candidates are always the largest province or the chief town of reference. I am referring, for example, to Lombardy 2, which you, Mr President, are well aware of: Brescia, Bergamo, Como, Lecco, Sondrio and Varese. In that circuit to be able to elect a candidate from the province of Sondrio or from the province of Lecco all the lists must really struggle. If with Porcellum the parties could even choose a territorial representativeness, possibility that has never been exercised, because they simply included those who wanted to get elected first, with preferences is even worse, in some ways, and even more with this pinball effect that could come to create and that, I repeat, cannot be solved by any technical solution. [[Applause from the M5S Group.]]

ParlaMint-IT_2015-01-22-LEG17-Senato-sed-382.ana.u184	DE PETRIS (Misto-SEL). Mr President, this amendment once again addresses the issue of gender preference. It is a long debate, the debate on the introduction of gender preference, which we had even during the examination of the measure on the European elections. However, this issue deserves further consideration, which I ask the Presidency to be able to do, with regard to the problem of which preference would be cancelled in the case of two preferences given to persons of the same sex. Now, it is clear to everyone that with respect to the choice that in the case of preferences given to persons of the same sex only the second is canceled, it is impossible that the voter, when writing the list of two preferences, intended to establish a hierarchy. This is in fact a fact all to be demonstrated; I repeat, in this case the will of the elector is frankly imponderable, considering that we could find ourselves in the unfortunate situation in which the elector ignores what can happen if he precedes for example Monica Cirinna to Loredana De Petris, where it is possible that those who may have provided during the campaign of propaganda cards, cards and posters instead wanted to cause damage in the formulation of the second preference. For this reason, with our amendment, we have indicated the most rational way, in my opinion, of nullity of both preferences, because in this way we would really guarantee fairness in treatment, otherwise we might even find ourselves in the unfortunate situation where the second name, in some way, is placed to identify the vote. Hence, from this point of view, there is also a question of extreme delicacy. There has been no world in the Commission to discuss this issue, since we have not been able to discuss the amendments. I therefore also call on President Finocchiaro to pay attention to this issue. I would also ask for the possibility of a provision of the sub-amendment for serious consideration of these sensitive aspects which I have previously brought to the attention of this House.

In Italy we have imported them for just over ten years and we have benefited from it, who does not remember the surprising victory of Vendola in the primary Apulian of 2005 and then of Prodi, up to the candidacy of Matteo Renzi to mayor of Florence in 2009? That is why I have tabled an amendment calling for a government regulation to fully regulate the conduct of the primary elections, which the electoral law in principle must demand. ParlaMint-IT_2015-01-22-LEG17-Senato-sed-382.ana.u213	D'ANNA (GAL (GS, LA-nS, MpA, NPSI, PpI)). Madam President, I believe that Senator Ricchiuti's amendment introduces a clarification of the basic aims of this bill, which is essentially a kind of negative prejudice to popular sovereignty. Plato, imagining the government of the best, or philosophers, also expressed a similar concept. But we are not in the Athens of the fifth century: we are in a modern society, post-industrial, in which the principle of equality was completely introduced at the time of the election of the constituent Assembly, when women were also able to exercise the right of passive electorate. What Senator Ricchiuti introduces with this amendment is a sort of corrective to a law that, not only assigns to minority parties the task of designating, but - thanks to the cleverness, the intelligence of those who built this legislation - will also allow those who go beyond 20% to be able to carousel with multiple capilists, blocking within the parties the minority components, or not completely in line with the will of those who, at that time, hold the management of the party and, therefore, the lists. That's good to be clear. Just have 30 or 40 trusted ascaries and put them in colleges where the political opponents are stronger, because they are more present on the territory and make sure that they do not resign, making the ones who are behind take over. Therefore, the expectation that this Parliament is for the three fifths made up of nominations can also be held to be in default, and not in excess. But I have to say that, in Italy, there is a kind of cultural bovarism, that is, a attachment to the fashions and practices in use in other countries. The American primaries - no one overlooks this aspect - are codified and controlled by the State, which has the perfect knowledge of those who sign up on the electoral lists and who then participate or sign up for political parties. Therefore, the electoral audience is known, because any democratic selection in which the electoral audience of those entitled to participate in the choice is not fixed is merely an infancy. 12:59 p.m. (Follow D'ANNA). And it is this limit that has determined the frauds of the Chinese or non-EU countries, which have polluted the primary ones in Naples, some time ago, and those in Liguria, a few days ago. Together with the Senators Compañera and Sposetti, I have allowed myself to go to the archche of the situation: the law governing the internal democratic life of the parties, presented by Don Luigi Sturzo in 1954 and which, according to the Mortati report made to the constituent Assembly, did not give rise to this type of subjection of the parties to the jurisdiction and empire of the State, because the Constituents came from twenty years of dictatorship and wanted the parties to be completely disengaged from the control of power. Here it is not a question of imagining the intervention of the State in the life and programmes of the parties, but of the intervention of the State which controls that the parties observe what they have written in their statutes from the point of view of democracy and the choice of their leaders, who tomorrow will be the leaders of the State. Since the office does not sanctify people, who is elected secretary or top executive of a party with a scam method because it is not codified and unverified by a third party, why should it become a man of state who has legality as the reference point of his government action? Amendment 1,7000/203, therefore, we will vote for it, but it is not a panacea, until in this nation the parties, which catch tens of millions of euros, will not become subjects of public law and which will have to deliver on February 15, which is coming, their statutes to the Chamber of Deputies, because this expressly provides for the law on the public financing of the parties. These parties receive money from the State without taking into account the perfect observance of what they have written in these statutes. A parliamentary system which, bon gré mal gré, is based on the point of mediation between society and the State through the forms of party and which leaves these forms, which are not legal forms at the level of the circle of hunting or bowls, how can it ever work and how can it accept in itself the system of democratic selection? You see, Hegel said: "What is real is rational."Well, if we ended up in the second Republic, in the personified party, in the party that refers to a person, it is because there is no structure of a party organization that observes the norms that are given. Therefore, it is useless to crucify Minister Boschi. Minister Boschi here is the deer who must protect not the Nazarene pact, but the system that allows his dante to make hefty parliamentarians of the PD who stand in his boxes. ParlaMint-IT_2015-01-22-LEG17-Senato-sed-382.ana.u235	CRIMI (M5S). Mr President, I imagine you have skipped all the amendments, from amendment 1,7001/136 onwards, but also the previous ones, because they are not included in the Esposito amendment. In fact, these amendments - and that is why I would ask you to do so - had a second part which cannot be precluded. I would ask you, if possible - and I am going to go into detail now - to vote separately for the subsequent amendments of this type and to vote at least for the second part, which is an additional amendment proposal and cannot be precluded by the Esposito amendment. The Esposito amendment, in fact, provided for a threshold above which the majority premium is given. In this case, however, all these amendments (to scale: they are all progressive and serial) have an important addition to them, which nevertheless identifies a minimum percentage, compared to those entitled to vote, that those who reach the threshold for having the majority prize must have. If you will allow me, Mr President, I shall explain the importance of the second part of these amendments and ask that it be put to the vote, at least in its initial part, or set aside and left standing, so that we can deepen and find a point of fall shared by everyone. We have, in fact, said - and we have been told by the Constitutional Court - that the majority prize is unconstitutional, because of all the distortions it causes. To make up for this, considering that 50% of voters go to vote, obtaining 40% of 50% of voters means 20% of the electorate as a whole. It has been established that a list with 40% should be given the majority prize; it would be appropriate and common sense to link this prediction - and here there is no obstructionist end, but it is an addition - to a minimum percentage, a threshold, with respect to the number of voters. I would remind you that absenteeism and abstaining from voting is an important issue. In this way, the norm would involve all political forces to avoid pushing on abstentionism, but, on the contrary, it would induce them to push for greater participation. It would be a stimulus, because a list that reaches 40%, with a set of voters of 50%, would not have the majority prize. It would therefore be in the interests, in particular, of the majority party, according to the polls, to ensure that more voters go to the vote so that their percentage is calculated on a voting body as broad as possible. Therefore, I would ask that all subsequent amendments of this type (they are serial, start from a percentage of 18 and provide for various percentages) be voted on by separate parties or, in any case, that part be disemboweled and perhaps set aside (since we have set aside others) and dealt with with with a short debate among the Groups on the subject, finding an element of fall, a minimum percentage, which, in my opinion, could be 40% of 70% of voters, assuming an increase of voters from 50% to 70% of voters. This could be the optimal drop rate, so as to push to increase the participation of voters from at least 50% to 70%. Of course, our expectation would be 100%, but we know that it is not everyone's expectation. It is a proposal, therefore, as moderate as possible, so that it can be accepted.

ParlaMint-IT_2015-01-22-LEG17-Senato-sed-382.ana.u247	DE PETRIS (Misto-SEL). Madam President, I rise on these amendments because they raise the issue of the award of the majority prize. Some of our amendments linked the award of the majority award to a threshold, which we proposed in the amendment itself to raise to 50% plus one and which proposed an indication of a percentage even for those entitled to vote. PRESIDENT. - The debate is closed. Please, if you lower your voice in this House, it's better for everyone. DE PETRIS (Misto-SEL). It's nothing imaginative. I would like to remind you that this issue raises a question because we could find ourselves in a situation in which the majority prize is awarded to a force that is actually very minority in relation to the electorate for the growth, alas, of the phenomenon of abstentionism, which we have repeatedly referred to as a dramatic issue of our democracy. Those amendments and also the assumptions presented by Senator Crimi have not been taken into account. We are proposing again the issue that is even more serious than the ballot. The statistics of the ballots, which have occurred since the direct election of the mayors and presidents of the Province, when there were, tell us that participation in the ballot is very often very low. It goes down even further. This means that the award of the majority prize becomes absolutely unreasonable because we could be faced with one case, which can be easily verified, so two forces go to the ballot, one of which has reached about 38 percent and the other not even 20 percent. Participation in the vote in the first round may be 50% - not even the majority - and at the ballot wins perhaps the force arrived second with a participation in the vote of 35 or 40 percent. I'm not making unrealistic scenarios, but very realistic. The last elections of Emilia Romagna already say that with only one turn there was a participation in the vote very low. These amendments make it possible to propose a rule, present in the ballot round of the elections of the President of the French Republic, which, in addition to indicating that he takes the prize who wins the ballot, indicates the need to have a minimum percentage, which we indicate, of valid votes compared to all those entitled. I repeat that this is a very serious matter. In judgment No. 1 of 2014 (which we have not called back for a few days) the Constitutional Court said that we cannot take the majority prize because it is an unreasonable and disproportionate principle. Many have interpreted this as a direct indication by the Court that the threshold is fixed. In fact, having introduced the system of balloting, in my opinion, the Court's ruling on the threshold is being bypassed, because the majority prize is awarded, to the balloting. All the more so, I believe that we need - and this is the proposal of our amendments - a certain percentage of those entitled. Otherwise we would be faced with a force that takes the majority prize without representing more than 10% of all voters. [[Senator Crimi's nodding his desire to intervene.]]

ParlaMint-IT_2015-01-22-LEG17-Senato-sed-382.ana.u273	CRIMI (M5S). Unlike what is indicated in the current wording of the law - I am sorry that Mr Falanga is not in the Chamber, who has raised an issue that I believe should be dealt with seriously by this House - here we are talking about the beginning in a clear way. In my opinion, this amendment is much more correct than the current wording of the draft law. I therefore call on my fellow signatories of amendment 1.7001/505, starting with my colleague Mrs D'Adda, to support amendment 1.7001/506, which provides - for your information, because it is four lines and not all of you have the file in hand - that from the entry into force of this law and until 30 June 2016 the current electoral discipline applies on the date of entry into force of this law, So far it may seem similar to the current text of the bill. However, a few words are added: "as amended and supplemented by the decision of the Constitutional Court No 1 of 2014." In this way it goes black and white that until June 30, 2016 we vote with the current law, as amended by the Constitutional Court. I believe that it is common sense enough to try to introduce this amendment, which is a little clearer than a text which instead talks about the application of this law. I repeat the perplexity of the concept of the application of the law, which could be - and unfortunately experience teaches us that it is not even a distant hypothesis - the subject of urgent and emergency interventions by the Government (decree or other), which can touch the part relative to the application. On this occasion, I would like to point out that the amendment proposed by Mr Falanga, which introduces not the application but the entry into force of the law, should be considered. In fact, if the objective is clear and that is, why not secure from any interventions of this Government or - and here I open a not so imaginative hypothesis - another Government that could arrive in a few months? Because you assume this government lasts. But nothing is eternal and we also saw it with Letta: she had an eighteen-month program and it seemed that she had to work so much and serenely returned home within seven months. Serenely, maybe not in seven but maybe in ten could also go home - and we hope so - the Renzi Government, replaced by another Government as already subordained a bit by these transversal alliances. Another government could then come along, which, relying on this misunderstanding between application and entry into force, could intervene with some modification by means of a decree or other regulatory acts of a secondary nature. To prevent this, I would say to make the law in the best possible way to prevent this or the future Government, which will come within a few months, from being able to intervene with emergency acts that would not be appropriate on an electoral law. ParlaMint-IT_2015-01-22-LEG17-Senato-sed-382.ana.u276	URAS (Misto-SEL). Madam President, I am voting in favour of amendment 1.7001/506 and to clarify the following. We are in a rather anomalous condition, because we are voting an electoral law that is grafted on an unfulfilled institutional reform, so much so that the law that we are debating serves to elect only the representatives of the Chamber of Deputies, overturning the dynamics of the legislative production that instead would have wanted exactly the opposite, that is, first to complete the institutional reform and, after that, due to the outcome of the popular consultation, to approve an electoral law consistent with the reformed institutional system. That's not it. There is only one good reason: we could go and vote before the process of constitutional reform is completed and therefore we must have a valid instrument. It starts from the assumption that the mutilation, due to the decision of the Constitutional Court, of the previous electoral legislation makes that electoral legislation inadequate to a peaceful conduct of the popular consultation for the formation of Parliament. The objective of the law we are discussing is therefore to fill a legislative vacuum. Why do we have to fill the legislative gap? We cannot prevent the President of the Republic from exercising his power, because of the judgment that he can have of the quality and stability of the Government of the country and therefore we cannot reduce his powers because of the general interest. I say this because only two are the cases: we had foreseen, for example, the repeal of paragraph 13, because we wanted the rule that we are discussing to come into force immediately at least for the Chamber of Deputies and the Senate was worth what was there, with the charges that it also suffered. It is said here that, in the meantime, the rule that we must apply is exactly what we are facing in the current legal order. It says this in order to clarify - and this serves - that the provision that has been inserted cannot be the legislative transformation of a political pact for which we do not go to vote before 1 July 2016: that is to translate a political pact into a legislative provision, intervening on the effectiveness of electoral rules, knowing that we have a legislative vacuum, is a madness that has no value, which will be remembered in the annals of Parliament's nonsense, not even bearable with respect to the dignity of this House and not only of this House, but of the whole country. We cannot be those who translate the political pacts into normal, preventing the full exercise of the powers of the authorities of the State, starting with that of the President of the Republic that we are about to elect. I invite, so much space, to reason on this topic. Since we have to approve this law, beyond the huge number of amendments that have been tabled, which have, moreover, been quickly kangarooed so that there has not been a waste of time for not even a quarter of an hour, and there is not even an attitude of violent obstruction, because here we are working and voting in a very peaceful and very serious way, I call on everyone to think about this. We try not to ridicule Italian politics, so on the one hand we make a pact and this pact is translated into norm and becomes the law of the State also and despite the defects of constitutionality that it contains.

ParlaMint-IT_2015-01-22-LEG17-Senato-sed-382.ana.u318	AIROLA (M5S). It doesn't matter. It's the thought that counts. CRIMI (M5S). The amendment can be taken into account. I try to explain this, since the illustration could not be made, that there was no work in the Commission and that the files are limited here. The amendment proposes replacing the reference to the results of the last general census of the population for the calculation of the seats to be allocated to the districts with the results of the data compiled by the Ministry of Interior calculated on the number of resident citizens. It may seem a bit curious and difficult to achieve, but even in the municipal elections we see that it is often referred to a census of ten years before when in a municipality it has perhaps crossed the threshold of 15,000 residents, which leads to the introduction of the ballot or not to the ballot. Then, you have to wait ten years to get to get that city to go to the ballot or it could happen casually that by the tenth year one goes down below 15,000 and, then, for another 10 years you remain below the threshold scheduled for the ballot. This reasoning serves to indicate that it is appropriate that the calculation of the circumscriptions, considering also the continuous migratory flow that exists between the Regions in Italy for reasons of work and study, is done every year on the basis of the data elaborated by the Ministry of the Interior on the number of citizens resident at the time of the elections, with a precise temporal indication. Just as the municipalities have to draw up the electoral lists and do a whole series of tasks, the Ministry of the Interior at that time has the electoral basin because it is perfectly known on a precise date how many voters are registered in the electoral register for each municipality. Because otherwise one could not even know the number of the electoral body that - instead - we know to be published on the website of the Ministry of the Interior. This is known for every municipality and, therefore, is a perfectly known and early figure. There is a precise timetable for the municipalities. Therefore, the Ministry of Interior could process this data in advance. Obviously this would imply that the obligations that the municipalities must do before the elections are due to expire at least 30 days before (even if I do not remember exactly what the deadlines are for the municipalities) in order to be able to have a given at the time of the elections.

It should therefore be reworded further, referring to the registrations on the electoral lists of the municipality that it believes to be part of the 15,000 inhabitants because it has the members on the electoral lists relating to the people who are entitled to vote. This amendment refers to the inhabitants. We take account of the fact that the inhabitants are not always in line with those who are voters, so the text should be further amended, also in relation to this additional element, which should be re-evaluated. Senator Crimes, this is not the first time: it may happen that an elector is registered on the electoral lists and perhaps still resident in another municipality, because it has not yet been cancelled. This amendment should, therefore, probably be further reworded and further specified. All this - I have said so - beyond the assessment that the Government will make in relation to the opinion that it will issue.

ParlaMint-IT_2015-01-22-LEG17-Senato-sed-382.ana.u353	CRIMI (M5S). Even if this could be a trap amendment, perhaps for some Movement that is claimed to have no statute or anything else (this obviously was one of those predictable things), I don't think Senator Sposetti had that intention or at least so I want to hope. However, I would like to draw attention to what this amendment provides for, because my colleagues may not know. In essence, it provides for the deposit at the Ministry of the Interior, in addition to all the usual things (such as the symbol, the mark, the lists and the delegates), also of its own statute referred to in Article 3 of Decree-Law 28 December 2013 n. 149. It is clear that this statute can only be presented where it is envisaged that a political formation should have it, because that law provides that the statute is a necessary and essential element in order to be able to access electoral refunds; those famous electoral refunds - to remember this - that you said you had abolished, but that in reality you did not abolish and that still stand: so they are the same and indeed more than before. A few months ago, in fact, many citizens came to letters in which they were invited to allocate the 2 per thousand to a party. These letters arrived by registered letter or were delivered by employees and were paid with a figure of 9 million euros, of which you may have forgotten a little. You have tabled an amendment - I remember that amendment, which I believe was signed by the rapporteur, Mr Pagliari, because it is a reminder that I have well written - which provided for the allocation of EUR 9 million for the communication to citizens of the possibility of allocating 2 per thousand to parties, amounting to EUR 7 million for the first year. But of those EUR 9 million earmarked for communication, how many have actually been earmarked for this purpose? The answer to the question would be to look for in your budgets: the sum of the budgets of item "Communication to citizens of the possibility to allocate the 2 per thousand" should contain the total of 9 million to you. Because while someone sent letters and spent on stamps, many others did not, but they still received a few million euros to communicate this faculty to the citizens. Evidently they were used for something else. That's just to remember and urge a little memory in this Senate. Since we're releasing him, at least he stays in the record, too. We will therefore vote against this amendment, because it provides for the obligation to submit an instrument which is compulsory only for those political forces who want access to the shameful funding of the parties that a citizens' referendum has abolished. That article exists because that Constitution comes from an affair in which parties, all parties but one, have been erased from the political life of this country. We, Madam President, are voting for this amendment to remind us that there is a dignity that must not be removed, rather it must be defended. If we live this moment today, a little turbulent and a little difficult, and we're talking about an electoral law... It is therefore worth all of us thinking about how Article 49 of the Constitution should be applied, about its right to exist, about the need to give this country rules as well, so that political life can take place in an organized way and not in a confused way, as at this time, so that in political organizations a mechanism through which internal democracy is measurable is ensured, the exercise of the right of all to profess their idea without having obstacles and without being punished or penalized. Other regimes have given us the idea that if one has a different opinion, even within a party, he is struck. It was once isolated; in other parts of the moment it was not only isolated, but also imprisoned and, sometimes, even executed. So it still happens today. A democratic country is also measured through the regulation of the democratic life of its political organizations and it is an argument that we must not refuse. "Partito" is not a swear word; it is a word that indicates an element in which all of us have the right to take part in something, to have an opinion, to be able to express it in an organized way with others and we must remove from our vocabulary the insult to this word. It is part of our history, also of my staff, as well as of so many of you, and must be defended. Such a thing cannot be removed. ParlaMint-IT_2015-01-22-LEG17-Senato-sed-382.ana.u407	ZANDA (PD). Mr President, I take the floor because Senator and friend Ugo Sposetti called me into question. We have not been able to conclude the examination of the electoral law in the Commission; we have had the work of the Commission which, in their final phase, saw 17,000 amendments being discussed; we have therefore come to this House without the rapporteur and without a text approved by the Commission. Several times in private conversations I have said something that I want to repeat here in this House: in my memory, this is the first time this has happened. An electoral law in this way presents enormous difficulties, because I have to say that electoral laws for which agreements must be reached during the debates of the House are very, very difficult. It is also for this reason that, until now, I have tried to avoid increasing the difficulties of this House by means of acts which would open up unplanned and, above all, non-constructive stages of discussion. Senator Sposetti knows, because we have talked about it among ourselves and also because I have been talking about it several times in this House, what my opinions are on gigantic issues for democracy, such as the implementation of Article 49 of the Constitution, the need for democratic Statutes of political parties and, even earlier, what, in my opinion, is the central and fundamental role in a parliamentary democracy of political parties. I believe that this is a point in our Constitution that we must try to protect in every way: in a democracy the role of political parties is fundamental and therefore the rules that override the parties are fundamental. I have, however, perceived, in the debate in this House and also in the political conversations that we all and each of us have with our colleagues, that there are, at this point in time, many nuances of thought. I am not referring so much to the issue of whether we need a rule that concerns the Statutes or a rule that regulates the functioning of the parties, in accordance with Article 49 of the Constitution, as to whether this rule should have the wording that we are discussing and whether it is correct that it is put into the electoral law. I have sensed that this problem existed and therefore I have the concern that this issue, put in this way into question and voted in the Senate Chamber, at such a delicate stage of our work, could be negative and compromise a work that we are instead trying to carry out in the time and in the manner required and with the best results. It is only for this reason, Senator Sposetti, that I did not take the floor earlier; I am taking it now to reaffirm my firm conviction of the positive nature of this rule, but my reservation that it is put to the vote at a time when I do not have the certainty of a positive outcome. ParlaMint-IT_2015-01-22-LEG17-Senato-sed-382.ana.u408	He asked the government representative to intervene. You have the right to do so.
ParlaMint-IT_2015-01-22-LEG17-Senato-sed-382.ana.u409	Madam President, I would like to explain the government's initial opposition to this amendment, also in the light of the debate that has developed in this House on a subject that is particularly sensitive, as we have also seen from the speeches of the various groups. The initial considerations were essentially of the extraneousness of this argument to the specific theme of the electoral law. It is obvious that the amendment not only raises the issue of the presentation of lists, but more generally also covers - legitimately - the rules of democracy and the organisation of parties and, ultimately, the implementation of Article 49 of the Constitution. I therefore believe that this is a particularly sensitive subject, which perhaps deserves an overall deepening and an organic discussion elsewhere, and not closely related to the presentation of the lists. This is with respect to the initial opposition. Compared to the remarks that have then been raised by both Senator Sposetti and Senator Bruno about the possibility that, through this amendment, the risk that more subjects can present the same symbol is avoided, I am not entirely convinced that this text is decisive. It is obvious, in fact, that nothing forbids more subjects to have the same symbol and also a statute that, in some way, legitimizes them to present the symbol. Surely they are elements that need timely discipline, also in order to be able to establish who has a prior right to use that symbol, but - in my opinion - cannot be dealt with here, in the context of the electoral law. The Government's negative opinion was therefore intended to protect, from a certain point of view, also a certain democratic nature in the possibility of access to electoral competition and, therefore, to leave greater scope. Given that the debate has involved the Groups in a broad way and that this is certainly a matter for the lives of the Groups and the Parties, at this point I think we can return to this House and it will be the Groups that will assess this. [[Applause from Senator Quagliariello.]]

We also witnessed, during the last European elections, the use of flag candidates, who had great media consensus, in places where they had no connection, even with the territory. Therefore, we want to put an end once and for all to this nasty habit of running for office in a multitude of colleges. Everybody run for home. I would like to use a slogan, improperly used by the League for other types of situations, and which I do not share but which I use in this case: that everyone applies to their own home. ParlaMint-IT_2015-01-22-LEG17-Senato-sed-382.ana.u460	TOCCI (PD). Mr President, I would like to announce that I will not take part in the vote on the major amendments. I wanted to announce in good time, before we arrive at the major amendments that will decide the substance of this electoral law, that I will not participate in the vote. I believe I am interpreting the orientation, even with plural accents, of other colleagues with whom I have signed the sub-amendments, which, as a result, we have already withdrawn. I did not want to have a surprise effect; so I wanted to explain it in time, so that everyone can assess the practical consequences of this choice, and of course I also wanted to briefly give some political reasons. It is very sorry to decide to remain silent in this House. It is even more painful not to participate in the votes of our Group. I take this opportunity to say that I hope this veil of misunderstanding between us PD senators will soon fall. Non-voting is a silence that speaks: it is a silence that would like to contribute, even in extremis, to a common reflection among all the political parties. I was not convinced of the justifications for the Esposito amendment: with a generic text and without any autonomous preceptive scope, certain obstructive amendments were deleted, but they had a clear legal validity. It is an illegal procedure, which I believe has been adopted without prior investigation in the Commission. All this moves parliamentary law on uncertain and swampy ground. I dare not think for which institutional forces could be invoked in the future as a precedent; this time however it has served to block the discussion and to prevent an improvement of the law. Yet there were many expectations for this reform. From ten Italian public opinion waited for the cancellation of the Porcellum and the reconstruction of the direct and verifiable link between elected and elected, as it had been created with the uninomial colleges. The 2006 law is at the origin of the Italian political crisis: from that moment a delegitimation of the Parliament began until the controversy over the caste. We had the opportunity to change towards and instead wrote a disappointing, negative page of parliamentary activity. We have missed the opportunity to write a simple, clear reform capable of rebuilding the trust between voters and Parliament. [[Applause from Senator Simeoni]] In institutional reforms, the method is substance and with an amendment that is not legitimate, the path to a new form of government is opened, a debate closes almost twenty years to arrive at a sort of absolute premierate without counterweights, political or constitutional, just as our master Leopoldo Elia feared. There is an imbalance of legitimacy between the head of the government, who is strong of a direct electoral mandate, in clear contrast to the current constitutional reform, and on the other hand a weak Parliament, because to a large extent it is still composed of members of the party leaders. All this goes along with the political crisis rather than solving it. Election participation now tends to go towards or below 50% of voters, so a minority leader with 20-25 percent of the actual votes wins the stand, has at his will the guarantee bodies and the legislation on fundamental rights. All this can only radicalize the rejection of the electoral party that feels excluded and therefore centralize the detachment from politics. We are careful of artificial solutions that evade the construction of a deep and rooted consensus in the country. For twenty years the parties have reacted to the loss of votes with the increase of the majority prizes and there is a risk of forming majority governments within minority democracies. The strength of a government is like the courage of Don Abbondio: those who do not have it on the basis of a popular conviction, cannot give it through the electoral artifices that in the long run accentuate the distrust of part of the electorate. [[Applause from the Mixed-MovX Group]] Could these forcings be avoided and concluded? Yes, a peaceful parliamentary discussion and improved mediation were possible if there had not been a rigid and even flaunted bond of extra-parliamentary agreements. Even in voting procedures, mediation could be found. Just now we applauded Senator Sposetti's mediation. Among other things, I take the opportunity, on behalf of everyone, to wish him well: it is also his birthday. [[General applause]] Perhaps we could also listen to him yesterday, when he asked the minorities to withdraw tens of thousands of amendments and the majority to remove the maxims that gave rise to the so-called kangaroo. I'm addressing Senator Calderoli.
ParlaMint-IT_2015-01-22-LEG17-Senato-sed-382.ana.u461	Senator, I invite you to conclude, are provisional amendments.
ParlaMint-IT_2015-01-22-LEG17-Senato-sed-382.ana.u462	TOCCI (PD). Senator Calderoli, it would have been a nice gesture on your part, to make it up to you to be the director of the Porcellum, but also by the majority it would have been a wisdom. There is sometimes the impression that these minority obstructionisms constitute the alibi to create an exceptional situation. Today I even read on the front page that the coup would be attempted just because some senators have tabled amendments that want to strengthen the power of voters. The July climate returns, which prevented us from having a peaceful debate on the Constitution. There are two missed occasions, yesterday's and today's. However, I hope that there will be other opportunities in the Senate Chamber for a positive, constructive and improved debate on the legal texts, at least on these fundamental acts. We must not lose confidence in the peaceful confrontation in this House. It would be like losing self-confidence. [[Applause from the PD, M5S, Mixed-SEL and Mixed-MovX Groups.]]

ParlaMint-IT_2015-01-22-LEG17-Senato-sed-382.ana.u498	ENDRIZZI (M5S). Madam President, the electoral law with which Parliament was elected, starting from the 15th legislature, has aggravated the crisis of confidence in the parties. The process of removing citizens from politics is taking on the tone of a real revolt against what is now referred to as the caste of politicians. The power of decision, the real one, is now transferred to other bodies, very often completely unrelated to the national institutions and to the classical constitutional ones, while also the power of control of the activities of the governments has left the parliamentary classrooms to move to other places, mainly those of public opinion and communication. The event of this beginning of the century risks being the fallibility of the myth of representative democracy; the idea that modern post-industrial societies can be governed more efficiently and functionally by regimes that are no longer inspired by the classic model of liberal democracy. At a time when it is not clear where the scepter is, voters demand that they be given back, first of all, the power to freely choose their representatives. Sovereignty belongs to the people who exercise it in the forms and within the limits of the Constitution (Article 1): never again a Parliament of nominations. This is not the flour of my sack, but the incipit of the act Senate 432, of initiative of Senator Stefano Esposito. [[Applause from the M5S Group]]

I'm not doing this because you tell me, but because the Undersecretary has arrived. Mr Pizzetti, I do not know how you were able to give a favourable opinion on Amendment 1.12290, which I would like to frame, since for Trentino, as for Valle d'Aosta, a system different from that of the rest of the country has always been in place. Now we also bring for the House an election system substantially identical to that for the Senate. This means having eight colleges in the same constituency: four in South Tyrol and four in Trentino. The seats to be allocated will be those proportional that will come out according to the rules, the rules and the last census. As the population of Trentino-Alto Adige is just over a million people, being a 60th or a little more than the Italian population, the relationships normally, if there are 600 and few MPs to elect, are about 10 (the number and proportion match) the MPs to elect in that constituency. Without prejudice to the fact that there are 8 colleges and that there will be a recovery of the proportional on the Mattarellum style, it means that 2, maybe 3 (2 normally), will be the famous fished on the proportional list. In order to fish on the proportional list and as a majority voter the system, with a single-name college, means that you have to divide the votes of the winner or, in this case, the difference between the winner and the second of the second placed list, keeping only the residual votes. If this operation is not done, it means that, winning in all the uninomial colleges, it would automatically win even in the remaining ones, which have been designed to be attributed to the oppositions (minorities, if we want to see them as minorities, at national level, but that maybe in that circuit are not so, because they make appearances at national level with the parties that win). What are the South Tyrolean colleagues asking for? They demand that the remaining seats do not fall out of Trentino. This would be right, because the calculation and the count drawn up by the rule that is being passed could cause some residual seat to be moved from one region to another. And so far we're here. They also want the votes of that constituency to be part of the great national count to know what the list that won, which would be the list that takes the majority prize. And so far we're here. But if we go along with point (f), by amending paragraph 7, it would happen that two thirds of the seats, which would be the seats on proportional representation, would be assigned to the list that achieved the highest national electoral figure. That would also be given at local level a second national prize; and, since there is already the national prize, it would be a very serious mistake to say that that list, attached to the list that wins at national level, would also receive two thirds of those residuals that had to be the prerogative of the oppositions, just because the winner takes 8. We have to read it by reading what the previous paragraph says. At this point there is also an electoral dispute, because, if the remaining seats are to be attributed by the D'Hondt method (dividing the total of the votes of each list by 1, 2, 3... up to the competition of the number of Members to be elected, means that the D'Hondt has a seat already assigned (who has won in the single college) but, with the following paragraph 7, saying that in any case two thirds go to the list that has already won, a second prize would be awarded again. This goes against the D'Hondt system, which would say to attribute it to its own (dividing by 1, by 2, by 3, etc.); if there were so many different votes, already with the first system the whole would be organized. It's a big mess, and I don't know how the government said this mess could stand. In any case, I would ask for a split vote, because everything can go well until paragraph 6, but paragraph 7 absolutely does not go well. Therefore, if colleagues do not withdraw it or if the Government does not change its opinion, we ask that paragraph 7 be voted separately. [[Applause from the LN-Aut Group.]]

ParlaMint-IT_2015-01-22-LEG17-Senato-sed-382.ana.u515	CRIMI (M5S). Madam President, I rise on Amendment 1.12290 to raise two issues, one relating to the first part. It is said that the quick kitten gives birth to blind kittens. Well, the text approved in the Chamber of Deputies had a vice, which was noted in the hearings by numerous constitutionalists as well as by some senators: there was no provision for the possible supplementary election for the replacement in the single colleges, in particular in Valle d'Aosta, in the event of resignation or in case the elected member loses his status as a Member. Today, in fact, we find ourselves faced with an amendment to the situation and, with the first period, we have to resolve the issue which was not dealt with in the House at the time. After that, in amendment 1.12290 we see a complex way (defined in four pages) to indicate the elections in Trentino Alto Adige and in Valle d'Aosta, with many problems inside. The honourable Member who spoke before me has already highlighted one. Personally, I would like to stress the mechanism whereby votes in single and proportional colleges, however in Trentino and Valle d'Aosta, contribute to the determination of the national electoral figure in order to identify the party, the list or group of lists that it has won and that it will therefore be entitled to the majority prize, although they do not serve to identify the seats to be allocated. This already creates some imbalance in the calculation of votes. Added to this is the further allocation of two thirds of the seats. And two thirds is much more than 52 or 53 percent of the seats at national level, compared to the calculation made. This is therefore an amendment which presents many critical issues. Again, we are hastening to pass a law to satisfy the whim of those who want to fire her before the election of the President of the Republic, with the awareness that she will have to make another parliamentary passage and therefore could also be further modified. In fact we know that we will talk about it in a year and probably in the House in a year will change the majorities as they have changed this year so the law will be changed again. But it has become a habit to do so. It makes no sense to continue in this way, Madam President. This, like other key issues, should be permanently shelved. That is to say, all the necessary votes, which are now automatic, will be put aside all the unresolved questions and will be postponed to a more peaceful moment, when the President of the Republic has been elected and when it has occurred that the majorities are the same. Because the election of the President of the Republic will change the political scenarios of this country, it is useless Hiding it from us. The suspension of work on the electoral law that we asked for could only concern those issues that have remained outstanding. Before arriving at the final vote, then, to address calmly the amendments dealing with unresolved issues such as gender preferences, the flipper effect or issues affecting the Autonomous Regions. It would be appropriate to stop, so the law will enter into force on 1 July 2016: there is a year and a half of time before this law gives its effect. The haste is therefore dictated by the whim of those who want to issue his tweet at the end of the month, as every month, to say that against the "owls" it has made it. Too bad that he said it even a year ago and after a year we are still here, always with the electoral law, to change it completely and we will find ourselves perhaps in the House in a year with another electoral law and then a year later in the Senate with another electoral law, according to the whims of the government protempore. I want to repeat it to everyone, because it's fundamental. PRESIDENT. - The debate is Please listen to President Azzollini, because everyone has done a great job. Mr Azzollini (AP (NCD-UDC)). The rules of a procedural electoral nature are, in our opinion, even more at the basis of the constitutional rules of which, by virtue of the equal ordination with Article 81, one does not evaluate honesty. In our democratic constitution, elections are a sort of "urnorm": it is the one that gives rise to the constitutional form. That is why, as far as the procedure is concerned, they must not be assessed from the point of view of honesty. I say this now in clearer terms: if a nation wants to vote four times in a year, could it be argued that the vote is taken because there is no money? Hence the basic nature of compulsory expenditure, which is the nature of the fund. This is the general principle which I confirm here. We have examined all the amendments which, in any case, have been covered. In the light of the general principle that we supported in the Commission in its opinion on the text, which I broadly confirm here, we believe that all the rules on electoral procedure, whatever they may be, do not need to be covered. It is different, as we have seen, for those outside the electoral function: a classic example could be the quintuplication of the emoluments for the scruters; in that case the norm does not concern the electoral function, but the emolument of the scruter. The same applies to the amendments tabled by my fellow Members, as we shall see in a moment, who wish to increase the funding of the fund, as with the amendment by Senator De Petris that we have examined. There you simply evaluate the cover. My colleagues believe that the compulsory expenditure fund should not be 100, but 105. Well, they're covering it up, and we're only considering whether or not those five are covered. On the basis of this we express our honest opinion and never on the procedure, which is the foundation of the principle of a democratic constitution like ours; rather on rules which are limited to deciding what, in the opinion of the proposer of the amendment, should be spent on those elections. These are parliamentary amendments which are the same as those of the Government; therefore, Amendment 1,12306 has rightly been the subject of our examination. In view of this, Madam President, the matter is closed with the examination of these amendments. We have examined the following amendments and we express a non-obstacle opinion on amendments 1.7001/264, 1.0.12002, 1.0.12003, 1.0.12004, 2.0.45 and 3.115. All these are orderly, like I said. On the other hand, we express our opposition under Article 81 of the Constitution to Amendment 3.16 because of the absence of that fund in the Stability Act: it was wrong.
